# Assumption layers and proof boundaries

We prove contract and protocol properties against explicit lower-layer
interfaces. We must distinguish the rules of execution, the correctness of a
particular contract, and the conditions under which that contract can make
progress. This register starts with the current asset-bridge slices; it is not
yet an exhaustive classification of every historical theorem.

**No project axioms does not mean no assumptions.** The axiom audit checks logical
dependencies. Theorem hypotheses, restricted transition systems, abstract data
representations, and unproved connections to deployed code need separate review.

## Layers

| Layer | Responsibility | Current evidence here |
|---|---|---|
| Logic | Lean's kernel and the admitted logical principles | Checked proofs; the audit permits only `propext`, `Quot.sound`, and `Classical.choice`. Lean and Mathlib revisions are pinned. |
| VM | Storage, call frames, caller identity, value, rollback, deployment, and execution resources for a specific VM/version | No concrete VM semantics or VM-to-model proof in this package. |
| Solidity | Meaning of source constructs: arithmetic, ABI, modifiers, calls, exceptions, and evaluation order | Source-review obligations; no formal Solidity interpreter here. Library code, including OpenZeppelin initializers, is contract code to verify. |
| Build and artifact | The selected compiler preserves source behavior, and the deployed artifact/configuration is the one reviewed | Source pin recorded; compiler preservation and deployment identity remain open. |
| Contract representation | Actual storage and entry-point executions correspond to the local contract model | Local model proofs exist. Concrete correspondence remains open. |
| Contract composition | A dependency supplies precisely the behavior required by its caller | Some local components compose; real external-call/frame behavior remains open. |
| Protocol and environment | Authenticated cross-chain evidence, cryptographic binding, permitted configuration changes, and progress | Conditional abstract results and explicit open obligations; no whole-bridge closure. |

These are a dependency graph. A balance-update lemma does not require chain
finality or compiler correctness to be true about its Lean definition. Applying
that lemma to a deployed bridge requires the particular paths connecting that
artifact to that definition. Each chain can select a different VM profile.

## How to record a dependency

Use the IDs below in the contract README and relevant specification comments.
For each claim, record the provider, exact required law, scope/version, evidence,
and remaining obligation. Classify each item as one of:

- **Trusted foundation:** accepted outside this proof, such as the selected
  platform executing its specified semantics. State the trust explicitly.
- **Theorem premise:** a visible Lean hypothesis, with a provider theorem when
  discharged by another component. A success equation is a premise about that
  call, not an assumption that every call succeeds.
- **Model boundary:** behavior fixed or omitted by the definitions. It requires
  correspondence or an explicitly restricted claim when applied to real code.
- **Open correspondence:** a precise implementation obligation still to prove
  or review. Do not silently treat it as an established fact.
- **Proved:** a locally checked result with a reference to its theorem and scope.

An obligation may be proved at one level while its connection to deployment is
open. For example, initialization proves representation consistency in the local
model; it does not establish that an actual proxy starts in that model's state.

## Layer register

### VM, language, and build

| ID | Required law / evidence to supply | Status |
|---|---|---|
| `VM-STORAGE` | Storage words, fresh allocation, address separation, and persistent-state effects match the chosen execution profile. | Open concrete connection. `freshProxy` is currently a model definition. |
| `VM-CALL` | Caller, execution address, storage context, value, static calls, and proxy/delegate execution have the modeled meanings. | Open; track per call path. |
| `VM-REVERT` | A failed frame restores its state-changing effects, including those of its subcalls. Establish which caller frames also revert and which catch/continue. | Open concrete connection. The model's `Option` commit rule is separate. The handler's nested-frame rollback (`Contracts.InteropHandler.exec`, `MODEL-HANDLER-REENTRY`) is another such model rule. |
| `VM-DEPLOY` | Freshness, deployment success, resulting code, and address derivation for the selected VM. | Open; no generic Ethereum CREATE2 formula assumed for all chains. `DeployFresh` (`NativeTokenVaultBase/Specs/RegistrationUnified.lean`) states the law for one bridged registration as a premise: the deployed address is nonzero and carries no asset id, and mapped addresses keep their code. L1's ETH sentinel `address(1)` is mapped with no code check (`registerEthToken`), so for it the premise rests on CREATE2 addresses being keccak outputs, not on a collision revert. |
| `SOL-ARITH` | Each operation uses the intended width, conversion, checked/unchecked behavior, and established range bounds. | Token bounds are proved in the abstract model; source-operation correspondence is open. |
| `SOL-ABI` | Input/output decoding, byte layout, hashing preimages, and decoding failures match typed model values. | Open. `MintData` and `List UInt8` do not prove an ABI layout. |
| `SOL-CONTROL` | Modifiers, evaluation order, high/low-level call results, and try/catch follow the chosen source semantics. | Open source correspondence; initializer guards are library behavior, not a language guarantee. |
| `BUILD-PRESERVATION` | Exact solc/zksolc versions, optimizer, target, libraries, and relevant flags preserve the source semantics used. | Not proved here; pin and explicitly trust or discharge this boundary for a concrete claim. |
| `GEN-EMIT` | Solidity printed by `EraSpec/Codegen/Emit.lean` behaves as `Codegen.call`/`Codegen.deploy` say: one construct, one line (the table in `Emit.lean`), with mixed checked/unchecked statements and panicking error arguments refused. | **Trusted**, not proved — no Solidity semantics here. Evidence: `solc` type-checks every output, `scripts/emit-solidity.sh --check`, and Lean-interpreter-generated vectors replayed in `solidity/` (960 calls, all agreeing). |
| `MODEL-CALLS` | `Codegen.Calls` gives external calls, bodiless internal hooks, `bytes`, the length check, `abi.decode` of static types, `abi.encode` of words and `bytes`, `msg.value` and `block.chainid` the meaning its interpreter states. A `Machine` parameter says what a callee or hook does. No reentrancy: the only callee is a `Codegen.Lang` program, which makes no calls, and hooks cannot write the caller's storage. | Model boundary (a definition), reviewed against Solidity's documentation, not proved. No emitter exists for these constructs, so `GEN-EMIT` does not cover them. |
| `BUILD-IDENTITY` | Full source/dependency pins and build inputs identify the runtime code, proxy/beacon implementation, initialization data, and configuration being verified. | Source context pinned at `c67894b970a0dac6fe9144d8ebf4b0806e15a9af`; no deployed-artifact claim yet. |
| `VM-HOOK-CALLER` | ZKsync OS's `L1_MESSENGER_HOOK` (`0x7001`) records a log only when its immediate caller is the messenger at `0x8008`, and then records the message log of the 20-byte sender and the message; `INTEROP_COMMITMENT_LEAF_HOOK` (`0x7004`) records only for the commitment tree at `0x10012`, and its log is not a messenger log keyed by `L2_INTEROP_CENTER_ADDR`. Documented at `protocol-docs/system/system-hooks.md:19, :22, :53-55` (`draft/v0.35.0` head `b17415d20`, unchanged at main `70096e2ea`); the constant's own comment (`common/l2-helpers/L2ContractAddresses.sol:18-19`) states no caller restriction. | **Theorem premise** `HooksAsDocumented` (`Contracts.L1MessengerKeying`, `L1Messenger/Specs/SenderKeying.lean`), not proved here: the hooks are ZKsync OS code. In `Protocol/L1WithdrawalProvenance` it is taken only on the chains the L1 run's committed verifications name (`HooksAsDocumentedOn`). Load-bearing: `UngatedHookForgesACenterKeyedLog`, and end to end `Contracts.L1WithdrawalProvenance.UngatedHookDeliversUndispatchedCalls`. The leaf-log clause is a format assumption the documentation does not state. That no other ZKsync OS component writes a log with sender `0x8008` is the closure stated in `MODEL-L2-MESSAGE-KEYING`. |

Solidity's documented distinction between propagated exceptions and caught or
low-level failures is essential to `VM-REVERT`/`SOL-CONTROL`: a failed subcall
does not by itself imply that its caller's earlier writes disappear.
See [Solidity error handling and arithmetic](https://docs.soliditylang.org/en/v0.8.28/control-structures.html).
This documentation version is a reference, not a claim about the compiler used
by a particular deployment.

Choose an explicit VM and protocol version before discharging these laws.
Native EraVM deployment and address derivation differ from Ethereum; the EVM
interpreter is another execution profile. See
[ZKsync's VM differences](https://docs.zksync.io/zksync-protocol/era-vm/differences/evm-instructions).

### Model choices and contract correspondence

| ID | Existing model boundary | Obligation for a concrete claim |
|---|---|---|
| `MODEL-LEDGER` | Token balances are `Address → Nat`, with a finite mathematical sum over all 160-bit addresses and a supply bound. | Relate these values to actual mapping slots and uint256 operations; the sum is a ghost invariant, not a runtime loop. |
| `MODEL-FRESH` | `freshProxy` has version 0 and an empty token ledger; the implementation has disabled version 255. | Justify the allocated proxy's relevant initial storage and the modeled initializer context. |
| `MODEL-COMMIT` | `transact` commits only `some`; on `none` it returns the original projected state. | Connect each modeled failure to the correct concrete reverting/caught frame. |
| `MODEL-TRACE` | Preparation appends one ghost obligation; ordinary transfer preserves pending entries. These are the only trace steps. | Show production publication creates that same obligation once; include or frame every other permitted action. This trace has no delivery or refund step; `MODEL-RESOLVE` adds them. |
| `REP-TOKEN` | The initialized nonlegacy token branch has modeled mint/burn/transfer behavior. The reinitializer path (`BridgedStandardERC20/Specs/Reinitialization.lean`) and the legacy zero-vault fallback (`MODEL-LEGACY`) are modelled locally. | Per-entry-point state/input/output correspondence, authority, arithmetic, inherited hooks, and failure paths. Allowance and code-upgrade paths need their own coverage. |
| `MODEL-LEGACY` | `BridgedStandardERC20.LegacyEnv` fixes the value `L1_CHAIN_ID()` returns for a run and assumes the call does not revert. The deprecated `l2Bridge`, `reinitializeToken`, `bridgeInitialize` and token upgrades are not steps. A legacy token's vault registry entry is taken as given (`Valid`): it was written before the pin, and the pinned source has no legacy setter. `prepareLegacy`'s reduction to `prepare` assumes `HookIgnoresTokenConfig`. Along the deployed runs of `ReinitializationStrong`, which do have `bridgeInitialize` and `reinitializeToken` steps, at most one committed call of a run from a nonzero version reads `L1_CHAIN_ID()` (`ReinitializationFaithful`: `DeployedLegacyBranchCountExact`); a run from a fresh proxy can commit two such calls, and the fixed value then takes both to read the same (`DeployedLegacyBranchTwiceFromFreshProxy`). | Justify inherited legacy registry entries (`OPS-CONFIG`) and the external call's result. Connect the modifier's pre-`require` writes and their rollback to `VM-REVERT`/`SOL-CONTROL`. |
| `REP-INIT` | Vault registration allocates, initializes with the vault caller, checks the address, installs both registry maps, then calls the tracker. | Match the actual allocation/caller/control flow, resolved origin, independently stored fields, and omitted origin/index/count writes. The three registry-only registration conditions follow from normalisation plus `DeployFresh` (`SourceInputGivesRegistryOnlyGuards`). |
| `REP-VAULT` | Preparation selects the tracked token, checks the reverse map, accounts before burn, and reads origin/metadata after burn. | Match the real branch, exact calls and ordering, concrete payload bytes, state frame, and propagated failures. Done at interpreter level for a transcribed program (`Codegen.VaultPreparationSlice`): both directions with exact returned bytes and logs, and the source's check order and revert data for the first four checks and both slice edges. The original vault bytecode stays open. |
| `REP-RESOLVE` | `bridgeMint`/`bridgeRecoverFailedTransfer` on the registered bridged branch: the three modifiers, the hook on the pre-mint token, then the token's `bridgeMint` to the data's `remoteReceiver` (mint) or `originalCaller` (recovery). The first bridge is `registerFreshBranch` with the data's origin token, then hook, then mint. `HooksCancel` (the tracker's inverse law) is a premise of the round trip only. | Match the real branch, call order, decoded fields and propagated failures, and relate the expected deploy address and resolved origin chain to the registration state. Native, legacy-token and base-token branches, and recovery's deploy branch, are not modelled. The L2 tracker's L1-destination counters do not cancel (`L2AssetTracker.sol:244-249, 299-304`), the case `RoundTripNeedsCancellingHooks` models. The hooks at the pin are supplied in `TrackerHooks` (`MODEL-L2-TRACKER`): the L1 ones satisfy `HooksCancel`, the L2 ones only `HooksCancelModulo`. |
| `REP-PUBLISH` | Pending entries pair the returned mint tuple with outer destination/asset context. | Match router/center publication, authentication, and replay identity; these are not fields of the returned tuple. |
| `MODEL-RESOLVE` | `AssetBridgePreparation` `Resolution` (`Protocol/AssetBridgePreparation/Specs/Resolution.lean`, `c67894b9`) keeps a source and a destination `NativeTokenVaultBase` state and the trace's entries. Delivery is `bridgeMint` and refund is `bridgeRecoverFailedTransfer`, both on the registered bridged branch (`REP-RESOLVE`), called by the chain's router with the entry's asset id and mint data verbatim. Each is guarded only by its own chain's record, keyed by entry position (`delivered` for the handler's `bundleStatus`, `refunded` for the manager's leg state), and by `ResolutionGate`, a lifetime predicate on the witness it consumes. One destination chain. Omitted reject-only checks: the handler's pause, source-chain, destination-chain, base-token and execution-address checks; the router's sender, selector and base-token asset checks; the manager's `recovered != 0`. Other writers (pause and admin actions, registrations, other assets, the destination's other mint routes) are not steps. `ResolutionEvidence` instantiates the gate with `Contracts.Refund`'s logged manager runs over a fixed `System`, and with one `MultichainGate` snapshot. | Show that each burn is published as exactly one bundle leg (`REP-PUBLISH`; the leg routing is a parameter), relate the position-keyed records to `bundleStatus[bundleHash]` and `_state[flowId][bundleHash]`, and show that the destination router's handler for the asset is the vault. Several destinations need the handler's destination-chain check. `TwoChainRefundAvailable` is enabledness in the model; the omitted checks must pass too. |
| `MODEL-ESCROW` | `Contracts.NativeTokenVault` treats `escrowed` (the token's balance of the vault) as an exact counter. A fee-on-transfer or rebasing token is outside the model. The aggregate `bridgedOut` ledger has no chain index. | Relate `escrowed` to the token's actual balance; `_depositFunds` rejecting fee tokens is part of that. `PerChainIsolationFails` states the aggregate-only boundary (it takes no hash premise). |
| `MODEL-CTM-ROUTING` | `Contracts.ChainTypeManager` models the CTM's version and transition registry and each chain's facet routing at `feat/upgrade-simplification` commit `e561b9cc2` (a moving branch). The transition engine is assumed to run `DefaultUpgrade`/`BootstrapUpgrade` logic with `CTMTransition`'s derived cuts. `EngineGettersFaithful` states that assumption as the premise `EngineDerived` and shows it load-bearing. A pre-v35 chain's own crossing of an opaque legacy edge is an executor-level step only in `LegacyCrossingFaithful`. `requireReleaseRouting` is modelled by its documented meaning in `ChainRouting` and by its code in `LiveRouting`; the two agree for releases passing `validate()` on consistent diamond storage (`DiamondOk`, which every `Diamond.sol` writer preserves: `CountCheckExactAfterValidate`, `DiamondCutPreservesOk`). `validate()`'s duplicate-selector scan is proved exact (`DuplicateScanIffNodup`, `ReleaseWfIsTheDuplicateCheck`), and `selectors()` is a fixed function of the facet address. `MutableSelectors` (cited at `draft/v0.35.0` `b17415d20`) drops that assumption: each facet's answer is state, a transition's cuts are stored when it is built, and the headline holds with stable answers as an explicit premise (`ChainsRunTheirVersionsReleaseStable`) and fails without it (`MutableSelectorsSplitRouting`, `MutableSelectorsLeaveDepartingFacetRouted`). Rows are flattened per selector. Reject-only checks (validate, deadlines, timestamps, batches executed, settlement-layer order, callers) are omitted, except in `UpgradeTiming`, which adds callers, both timestamp gates (the manifest's and the `ServerNotifier`'s), batches executed and the settlement-layer order, and identifies every chain's `s.chainTypeManager` with the CTM. The positive `Enabledness` results are relative to the kept checks. `ExecutorUpgradeChain` models the executor's `upgradeChain` with its `oldProtocolVersion()` lookup, again without callers and timing. The delegatecalled `DiamondInit` and `L1GenesisUpgrade` are transcribed in `GenesisWriters`: `Step.genesis` and `Step.migrateIn` are what that code does (`CodeCreationIsAStep`), exactly for a validated release (`GenesisStepIsExact`, `MigrateInStepIsExact`), assuming `GenuineRelease` and that `Diamond.DiamondStorage`, the reentrancy lock and `ZKChainStorage` are disjoint. CTM code swaps (forwarded, or by an infrastructure row naming the CTM proxy) are modelled in `ExecutorForward` as an arbitrary rewrite of the CTM's version state and are excluded from the positive results (`ForwardCtmSwapBreaksInvariants`, `CtmSwapBreaksChainLocal`). Migration out and back is modelled in `ChainMigration` (a returning chain reuses its diamond, so drift is kept). Break-glass cuts are handled per chain (`ChainLocal`, `ChainLocalExecutor`). No step checks the diamond's dispatch (`DiamondProxy.sol:32-36`); that only adds runs, but makes the repair step's existence enabledness in the model only (`RepairStepIgnoresLiveRouting`). Legacy cuts other than the bootstrap's are opaque; the bootstrap entry state is the post-migrate state. | Not modelled: what swapped-in CTM code does; diamond storage written directly by a delegatecalled init, which can fool the count check (`CorruptStorageFoolsCountCheck`); chains on other CTMs; L2 effects. For a concrete repair, show that the drifted diamond still routes `executeUpgrade` to code that performs the cut, through a route not marked freezable while the diamond is frozen; for a concrete transition, that the departing release routes `upgradeChainFromVersion` likewise. |
| `MODEL-CTM-ENGINE-V35` | `Contracts.ChainTypeManager.ExecutorEngineAtV35` models `CTMUpgradeExecutor.applyInfrastructureUpgrade` at `draft/v0.35.0` commit `b17415d20` (`ProxyUpgradeExecutorBase._applyProxyUpgradeTransition`, which delegatecalls `executeUpgrade` on the engine the transition's manifest names; first written at `d9f6789`, re-read at `b17415d20`) over `ExecutorForward`'s state and steps. The other entry points are `ExecutorForward`'s steps: `forward` gained a code check on targets of calls with calldata, which never rejects a modelled call (they go to the CTM and its `ProxyAdmin`). The code at an engine address is an arbitrary relation on the model state, so every engine is covered. Added state is three ghosts read only by the executor's checks: its own `owner()` and `pendingOwner()`, and the ten values `_domainHolders` reads; the hash is an arbitrary function and results say "holders unchanged, or the logged pair (before, after) collides". The pre-check that the CTM's `admin()` is not the `ProxyAdmin` is a step premise. `DefaultProxyUpgradeEngine` is its admin-owner check and the `e561b9cc2` row library's `applyRows` on the manifest's rows between two hooks; the witness engines are sequences of `ExecutorForward`'s forwarded calls, and two write the ghosts so that the ownership and holders checks reject. `_checkPostconditions`, `onlyOwner`, the `toRows` length check inside the engine and `_domainHolders` reverting for a ServerNotifier the `ProxyAdmin` no longer administers only reject (the executor's own length check is a step premise). | The executor checks neither `EngineIsTame` nor `EngineReviewed`; for a concrete release they are obligations on its engine's code (`CuttingEngineBreaksTheHeadline`, `CtmSwappingEngineBreaksSysInv`, `BeforeHookDefeatsTheSourceCheck`), as the source documents (`ProxyUpgradeExecutorBase.sol:40-41`, `IProxyUpgradeEngine.sol:16-21` at `b17415d20`). Not modelled: ownership and admin transfers through `forward` (no step but an engine leg changes the ghosts); calls that touch none of the modelled state (ServerNotifier setters, events); the Core domain's executor, which shares the base. The CTM, its chains and its release and transition objects are `ExecutorForward`'s `e561b9cc2` model, not re-read at `b17415d20`. Inherits `MODEL-CTM-ROUTING` and `MODEL-ROWS` (`ReinitFrames`). |
| `MODEL-ESCROW-HEAD` | `Contracts.NativeTokenVault` topic `EscrowLedgerAtHead` (`main` `70096e2`, `draft/v0.35.0` `b17415d`; `L1NativeTokenVault.sol` and `NativeTokenVaultBase.sol` unchanged since `3605e82` and `d9f6789`): the pin's registry-and-escrow steps (`MODEL-ESCROW`) plus `populateBridgedOut` (`L1NativeTokenVault.sol:157-181` on `main`), one asset per step, with the skip of an already-populated asset and the native check of `legacyBridgedOut` (`:125-126`). What `legacyBridgedOut(a)` returns is a parameter `legacy a`, fixed for a run, and the call is taken to return it (its external reads and subtraction, `:143-154`, do not revert). Runs start from any state; the solvency results take `LedgerBackedAtHead` (ledger plus unpopulated legacy amounts at most the escrow) as a premise, and the in-place-upgrade start `UpgradeStartAtHead` carries it as `legacyBacked`. No hash premise except `LoggedRunSoundAtHead` (`IdAssumptionsOn` on the start's recorded pairs and the run's own). The `draft/v0.35.0` per-chain maps are not modelled; their writers do not write `bridgedOut`, `bridgedOutPopulated` or the escrow, and the per-chain cap only reverts (argued from source). `MODEL-INTEROP-POOL` (`Contracts.L1NativeTokenVault.InteropPool`) models those maps separately, with no theorem linking the two models. | That the legacy amounts are fixed and backed is a claim about deployed legacy state (the vault's `DEPRECATED_chainBalance`, the removed v31 tracker's L1 entry and the bridgehub's chain list), not checked by `populateBridgedOut` (`UnbackedLegacyBreaksSolvencyAtHead` shows the model's solvency needs it): `OPS-CONFIG`. Per-chain caps and their enabledness on `draft/v0.35.0`; `MODEL-ESCROW`'s obligations. |
| `MODEL-CTM-PROOFGATE-V35` | `Contracts.ChainTypeManager.ProofGateAtV35` and `UpgradeTimingAtV35` model `draft/v0.35.0` at `b17415d20` (first written against `d9f6789`): `ChainRouting` and `UpgradeTiming`, kept whole, plus each diamond's `secondProofSystemRequired`, `verifier` and `disabledProofSystems` and the checks #2628 added to `Admin._executeDiamondCut` and `BaseZkSyncUpgrade._setVerifier`. `getProofMode` is a fixed function of verifier and mask (`ProofWorld.multi`), a release's `verifier()` a fixed function of the release. `setProofSystemStatus` is an arbitrary write of the mask (its caller rule is omitted); callers of `permanentlyRequireSecondProofSystem` are modelled only in `UpgradeTimingAtV35`. Every break-glass cut makes the v35 `Admin` facet's final check; a chain's own crossing of an opaque legacy edge with a pre-v35 facet makes none, which matters only for a diamond that requires its second proof system and routes a pre-v35 facet. The fields belong to a diamond; migration in deploys fresh fields. | For a concrete claim, show that the deployed verifiers meet `DisablingNeverEnablesMultiProof` (`MultiProofVerifier` and `ZKsyncOSVerifier` do; `MultiProofTestnetVerifier` when its inner verifier does) and that the chain's diamond routes its upgrade entry points to the v35 `Admin` facet. The results are about the diamonds registered on one CTM. |
| `MODEL-CTM-PENDING-L2` | `Contracts.ChainTypeManager.PendingL2UpgradeAtV35` models, at `draft/v0.35.0` `b17415d`, each chain's pending L2 upgrade transaction, its upgrade batch number and verified-batch counter, the batch steps (commit, prove, execute, `_revertBatches`), the upgrade's L2 gates and effect (`BaseZkSyncUpgrade.sol:96-105, 162-202, 218-272`), and the CTM's stored deadline overrides as state with both writers (`setProtocolVersionDeadline`, and the legacy commit at the CTM's version). It reuses `UpgradeTiming`'s timed steps, whose gates were re-read at `b17415d`, and `ChainRouting`/`VersionRegistry` at `e561b9cc2`. It replaces `setAux` by the writers of each field and adds `_requireSameDeadline` as a gate of a replacement. Reject-only checks are omitted: the version shape, the L2 transaction's own checks, the verifier and second-proof-system checks, batch contents, and callers. `hasL2` (whether a transition's plan has an L2 side) and `l2Hash` (the composed transaction's keccak) are configuration; no property of keccak is assumed. A commit records batch `totalBatchesCommitted + 1`. The bootstrap crossing may write any transaction hash on a chain with no upgrade batch, with its gates omitted. Break-glass cuts move only the version and the routing. | `DiamondInit` with the genesis upgrade and the three `Migrator` writers are one arbitrary `reinit` write, allowed at the CTM's version or for a chain that settles elsewhere; the batch fields a migration copies from another diamond are assumed to satisfy `batch ≠ 0 → txHash ≠ 0`. Not modelled: a swap of the CTM's implementation, diamond storage written by delegatecalled code, chains on other CTMs. Reading `LateUpgradeLandsInDeadTime` at the source needs the composed transaction's keccak to be nonzero. |
| `MODEL-UPGRADE-AUTHORITY` | `RegistryBootstrapMigration` models ownership/nomination of the CTM and its `ProxyAdmin` and the bootstrap bundle at `e561b9cc2`; `EcosystemUpgradeExecutor` models the coordinator's stages, reservation and timer at `afa407085`. At `e561b9cc2`, `RegistryBootstrapMigration` `Forward` models the CTM executor's `forward` on the authority state: a covered forward is a run of the labels (`ForwardIsARun`), and `renounceOwnership` is outside the label set and strands authority (`RenouncedAuthorityIsStranded`). At `draft/v0.35.0` `b17415d20`, `RegistryBootstrapMigration` `AuthorityEngineV35` adds both executors' engine legs on that state: the CTM-admin pre-check, the executor-ownership check and the domain-holders hash, with `holdersHash` an arbitrary function; the CTM's admin role, notifier binding and notifiers are state. Along runs it assumes that swapped-in CTM code keeps the modelled entry points. | Omitted: CTM implementation swaps by the `ProxyAdmin` owner (in `Forward` an arbitrary rewrite, excluded from the positive results; `ForwardCtmSwapReparks`); `ProxyAdmin.changeProxyAdmin`; notifier handover and core-domain reservation; `setCTMExecutor`/`setCoordinator`; in the coordinator model, the CTM executor's own `forward` (which commits without the coordinator); arbitrary stateful timer code. In `AuthorityEngineV35`: `changeProxyAdmin` on the CTM proxy (so the state the pre-check guards is not reached), legs nested in the other domain's leg, the rows check, ETH and events. |
| `MODEL-CAO-AUTHORITY` | `Contracts.ChainAdminOwnable` (`ChainAdminOwnable/Specs/Authority.lean`, `b17415d20`) models a `ChainAdminOwnable`'s storage (owner, nominee, `tokenMultiplierSetter`), every ZK chain diamond's admin pair and the Bridgehub's owner, nominee, admin and pending admin with one execution relation `Sem`, in which unknown code may at any depth call anything as any address other than the `ChainAdminOwnable` and zero. Only a `multicall` iteration and `setTokenMultiplier` make calls whose `msg.sender` is the `ChainAdminOwnable`, and each logs its origin. A revert is the absence of a derivation; any call may fail with no effect. ABI decoding is a parameter; selectors are the real constants. A diamond's `setPendingAdmin`/`acceptAdmin` are exact, and its `onlyAdmin` and `onlyAdminOrChainTypeManager` functions keep their access check before unknown code; the Bridgehub dispatches on the 51 selectors of its committed ABI with no `fallback`. | Not modelled: diamond creation (the diamond set is fixed); the code a cut installs (a cut leaves the admin pair arbitrary, and the diamond's functions stay the `b17415d20` ones); the Bridgehub's `ProxyAdmin` and implementation upgrades (its code is fixed); on the diamond, the admin-gated functions' checks other than the access check, and their writes; the extcodesize check before `ChainAdminOwnable.sol:69`; ETH balances; other `ChainAdminOwnable` instances; the chain type manager's admin role. Tie `BRIDGEHUB_SELECTORS` to the deployed implementation (`BUILD-IDENTITY`). |
| `MODEL-ROWS` | `Contracts.ProxyUpgrade` models a revert as the absence of a step. Admin ownership is fixed configuration. The live implementation is one mapping, read as if each row names the admin that actually administers its proxy. `callInitializeUpgrade` and events have no effect. Which facets a release marks freezable is `OPS-CONFIG` (nothing in `Diamond.sol` or `CTMRelease` constrains it): with the cut entry points and `unfreezeDiamond` routed freezable, the model admits no change after a freeze (`FreezableEntrypointsMakeFreezePermanent`). |
| `MODEL-CAH-MIGRATION` | `Contracts.ChainAssetHandler` (`ChainAssetHandler/Specs/Migration.lean`, `draft/v0.35.0` at `b17415d20`; every cited file is byte-identical at `d9f6789`) models every hub at once: per hub and chain, the bridgehub's `settlementLayer`, registration and `chainTypeManager`, the handler's `migrationNumber`, its three pause switches, the CTMs' versions, and the chain's diamond there (version, own CTM, whether it is away); on L1, `isMigrationInProgress` and the `isActive` and `settlementLayerChainId` fields of `_migrationInterval[c][1]`. Migration messages carry source, destination, chain, CTM asset id, migration number and protocol version, with a delivery status. Steps: L1's `createNewChain` and `registerAlreadyDeployedZKChain`, any hub's `bridgeBurn` and `bridgeMint` (fresh or returning diamond), the failure of an L1 → settlement-layer transaction, L1's `bridgeConfirmTransferResult`, an L2's `setSettlementLayerChainId`, the six pause entry points, and environment steps raising a CTM's or a diamond's version. A revert is no step; reject-only checks are omitted (listed in the Specs header: admin checks, `isReadyForMigration`, the diamond's priority, deposit-pause and batch checks, base-token data, message-root bookkeeping, some `_validateChainParams` checks, the upgrade engine's bound on an away diamond's version). Whitelists, CTM asset-id registries, CTM registration and owners and handler owners are a fixed `World`; `CHAIN_MIGRATIONS_ENABLED` is a parameter (`false` at the pin). | Registry, whitelist, ownership and code changes during a run, a cut the CTM owner has the CTM execute on a diamond, and `registerAlreadyDeployedZKChain` registering a diamond not set up by `DiamondInit` (one that could start away) are `OPS-CONFIG`; message transport is `PROTO-CAH-DELIVERY`. Batch numbers and the intervals' batch fields, `isValidSettlementLayer`, priority trees, the deposit-pause timestamp and the message root are not modelled. Callers, storage, ABI and call correspondence are open. |
| `MODEL-EXEC-PIPELINE` | `Contracts.ExecutorFacet` (`ExecutorFacet/Specs/Pipeline.lean`) models one chain's batch pipeline at `draft/v0.35.0` `b17415d20` (every cited file is identical at `d9f6789`): commit, prove, execute and revert, priority-mode activation and deactivation, priority-op pushes, the upgrade engines' write of the L2 upgrade transaction (with `_requireAllBatchesExecuted`), `setValidator`, and the settlement-layer writes of a migration's start (`forwardedBridgeBurn`) and failure. Counters and batch numbers are `ℕ`. Reject-only checks and external calls (protocol version, DA validator, L2 messenger, `MessageRoot`, dependency-root lookups, interop-fee charge) are omitted, which only adds runs. `Env` fixes the stored-batch hash, the commitment, the rolling hash, the priority tree's root, `Merkle.calculateRootPaths` and the verifier for a run, none assumed injective; a verifier replaced by an upgrade is covered by reading `Env.verify` as "accepted when the call was made". Ghost logs record each accepted stored-hash check for a value other than the slot's preimage and each priority-op proof accepted for items other than the pushed ones. `Init` is the state after `DiamondInit`; its slot-0 preimage is the CTM's `batchZero`, numbered `0` (`ChainTypeManager.sol:329-347`). | Not modelled: the return of a migration (`forwardedBridgeMint` rewrites the counters, stored hashes, priority tree and upgrade fields), so the results cover a chain from `DiamondInit` until such a return; upgrade code other than the transaction write (`OPS-CONFIG`); calls whose unchecked loop counter wraps, which need `2^256 - totalBatchesVerified` batches in one call (`UncheckedIncExactBelowWrap`); which verifier accepted a proof; what producing an accepted priority-op proof for unpushed items takes for the real tree. Storage, ABI and call correspondence of the facets (`VM-STORAGE`, `SOL-ARITH`, `SOL-ABI`). |
| `MODEL-ROOT-STORAGE-V35` | `Contracts.InteropRootStorage` (`Contracts/L2InteropRootStorage/Specs/Storage.lean`) models `L2InteropRootStorage` at `draft/v0.35.0` `b17415d20` (byte-identical at `d9f6789`): both mappings as total functions, both writers with every guard in source order, `onlyCallFromBootloader` against `0x8001`, a batch as all or nothing, and runs of successful calls by any caller. `RootsAtC67`/`execAtC67` transcribe the `c67894b9` contract, which has no `latestInteropRootTimestamp`; its runs are the projections of this model's (`C67894b9RunsAreProjectedRuns`). The event is omitted (`ReachLog` records the calls). The results that need a start state start from fresh storage, which the source comment at `:29-31` asserts for the v31 upgrade; `LegacyRootBreaksZeroTimestampClaim` shows the zero-timestamp claim needs it. `RootImportLink` reads `Contracts.RootImport`'s `World.imported` off one `c67894b9` storage per destination, with blocks taken modulo `2^256`. | Connect the mappings to storage slots and the writers to the bootloader's calls (`VM-STORAGE`, `VM-CALL`, `SOL-ABI`). That the bootloader imports only the settlement layer's `historicalRoot` tuples, checked when the importing batch executes (`ExecutorFacet._verifyDependencyInteropRoots`), is `PROTO-EVIDENCE`; nothing here makes a stored root or timestamp authentic. The empty start state and code replacement by protocol upgrades are `OPS-CONFIG`. |
| `MODEL-MESSAGE-VERIFICATION` | `Contracts.MessageVerification` (`Contracts/L2MessageVerification/Specs/Verification.lean`) models `L2MessageVerification._proveL2LeafInclusionRecursive` with `MessageHashing._getProofData`, `parseProofMetadata`, `extractSlice` and `Merkle.calculateRootMemory` word for word at `b17415d20` (the same code at `c67894b9` and `main` `70096e2`), reading the storage of `MODEL-ROOT-STORAGE`, and `readSettlementLayerReference` (absent at `c67894b9`) for comparison with the verifier's anchor. The three hashes are uninterpreted parameters; no result assumes their injectivity. The self-call is a direct recursive call whose revert propagates, bounded by fuel (`FuelIrrelevant`). Consumers (`AtomicInteropProof`) are not modelled. | `getLeafHashFromLog` and calldata decoding (`SOL-ABI`), the external self-call and its gas (`VM-CALL`, `SOL-CONTROL`), and `L2_INTEROP_ROOT_STORAGE` being that storage contract (`BUILD-IDENTITY`). That an accepted path opens a tree, and its hash premises, are the `Merkle` folder's results (`CRYPTO-MERKLE-BUNDLE`, `CRYPTO-PAIR-INJ`); `CalculateRootMemoryIsMerkleSpecWalk` identifies the walk with `MerkleSpec.walkPure`. |
| `MODEL-NULLIFIER` | `Contracts.L1Nullifier` (`L1Nullifier/Specs/Model.lean`, `draft/v0.35.0` `b17415d20`; first written at `d9f6789`, at which every cited file is byte-identical) models the nullifier's storage (`depositHappened`, owner/pending owner, pause, initializer flag, the three set-once addresses, every deprecated slot) and its twelve state-changing entry points, plus the router's pause flag, a direct call of the router's `bridgeConfirmTransferResult`, and the bridgehub → router → nullifier record chain of `requestL2TransactionTwoBridges` as one step. A revert is no step; `ExternalSender` excludes `0` and the three system contracts as top-level callers. Calls nested in a claim's call to the router are `Specs/Reentrancy.lean`'s `NestedRun`: with the source's delete-before-call order every nested execution is a run (`NestedRunIsFlatRun`). `nonReentrant` is not modelled (its checks only remove executions). The router's `_checkAssetHandlerAllowed`, the asset handler, the bridgehub's own checks and `_sendRequest` are reject-only and omitted (more runs). A `l1AssetRouter` other than the router is an unmodelled contract that may accept. `encodeTxDataHash` is an arbitrary function. | Storage/call correspondence (`VM-CALL`, `VM-REVERT`); the asset handler's refund itself and its accounting (`DEP-RESULTS` for `UnclaimedDepositRecoverable`); the router's owner and its pause (modelled as free); proxy upgrades and the immutables (`OPS-CONFIG`); the deployed proxy's actual records (the model starts from arbitrary storage). |
| `MODEL-MR-DIM` | `Contracts.MessageRoot` (`MessageRoot/Specs/Model.lean`, `draft/v0.35.0` `b17415d2`) keeps each chain tree (`DynamicIncrementalMerkle.Bytes32PushTree`) as the list of leaves pushed since `setup`, plus a ghost count of `setup` calls. The tree's root is `bytes32(0)` with no leaves and an uninterpreted function `chainTreeRoot` of the leaves otherwise; `chainTreeLeafCount` is the list's length. A `push` into a never-set-up tree reverts, as `DynamicIncrementalMerkle.sol:95` does. `ChainTreeSetUpOnce` shows the count is `1` for registered chains and `0` for the others on reachable storage, the only case the abstraction is meant for. | The library is not transcribed. Show that on a tree set up once `push` does not revert (below `2^256 - 1` pushes) and returns, and `root()` reads, one function of the pushed leaves, and that `_nextLeafIndex` counts them (`VM-STORAGE`, `SOL-ARITH`). |
| `MODEL-MR-ENV` | Each `Contracts.MessageRoot` call sees its own `Env`: `msg.sender`, `block.number`, `block.timestamp` and the answers of its external calls (`getZKChain`, `owner`, `getAllZKChainChainIDs().length`, `chainBalanceBackfillPending`, `isSecondProofSystemRequired`, `getProofMode`, `l2LogsRootHash`), free per call; block numbers and timestamps need not increase along a run. No external call reverts, so the model admits successful calls the contract would revert; the safety results are over that larger set, and `AppendLiveness`/`AddNewChainLiveness` make no external call besides the Bridgehub's `getZKChain` view. Of the verifier only the final-node branch is modelled (`finalNodeAccepts`), with the parsed proof as an input. | Relate each answer to the Bridgehub, vault and chain diamond actually called (`VM-CALL`, `DEP-RESULTS`). `MessageHashing._getProofData` and the settlement-layer recursion (`L1MessageRoot.sol:96-120`) are open. `L2MessageRoot` is not modelled. |
| `MODEL-MR-GENESIS` | `Contracts.MessageRoot.Reachable` starts with a successful `initialize` (`reinitializer(2)`; "used in local deployments", `L1MessageRoot.sol:62`) on all-zero storage, with no call before it. | A proxy upgraded from an earlier release is outside `Reachable`. For it, show that its storage satisfies `Inv` at the upgrade, after which `InvKeptByEveryCall` applies; this is not proved. Calls on a fresh proxy before `initialize` are not modelled (`OPS-CONFIG`). |
| `MODEL-VALIDATOR-TIMELOCK` | `Contracts.ValidatorTimelock` models every state-changing entry point of the `ValidatorTimelock` proxy at `draft/v0.35.0` `b17415d20` (`ValidatorTimelock.sol`, `AccessControlEnumerablePerChainAddressUpgradeable.sol`, and OpenZeppelin v4.9.6 `Ownable2StepUpgradeable`/`Initializable`): the owner, the three delay setters, the per-chain role registry with the six rotation wrappers, and the five forwarding entry points. A revert is the absence of a step. What one call reads from outside the timelock (`block.timestamp`, the chain's `getChainId()` and `getAdmin()`, `BRIDGE_HUB.getZKChain`, the forwarded call's success) is a per-call `Env`, unrelated between calls; a call the chain makes back into the timelock during the forwarded call is a later call of the run. `msg.sender` is any word. Gas is not modelled, except that the non-terminating loop at `_processBatchTo == type(uint256).max` is excluded. `committedAt` is the packed `LibMap.Uint32Map`, transcribed bit for bit (`Specs/LibMap.lean`; `PackedTimestampsRefineRuns`). | Not modelled: the chain diamond (whether it accepts a forwarded call, and its check of the forwarded range against its batch data), `MultisigCommitter` and `PermissionlessValidator`, events, views, and upgrades of the timelock's code (`OPS-CONFIG`). The delay results bind only batches committed through the timelock, with the commit time below `2^32` (`ExecutionWaitsForTheDelay`; `UnrecordedBatchIsNotDelayed`, `ClockPast2106SkipsTheDelay`). That no call has sender `0`, a premise of `RenouncedTimelockOnlyRaisesDelays`, is `VM-CALL`. |

`EraSpec/Refinement.lean` names obligations O1–O9. Their
primary representation owners are: O1 tree storage/updates, O2 insertion guards,
O3 bounded search, O4 manager transitions, O5 caller authority, O6 source-chain
binding, O7 root correspondence, O8 the all-legs gate and flow-id check, and O9
the vault and router models. They require the applicable VM, language,
build, dependency, and cryptographic laws above; naming an obligation does not
discharge it.

### Dependencies, evidence, and operations

| ID | Required law | Current boundary |
|---|---|---|
| `DEP-FRAME` | A dependency's calls/callbacks can change only the modeled state, or all further effects are represented and preserved. | Hook types restrict writes to `Accounting`; that restriction is not a proof about real callbacks or reentrancy. The token getter is a local field read. |
| `DEP-RESULTS` | The particular dependency results required for enabledness are available. Concrete tokens, deployers, metadata, encoders, and trackers meet their declared interfaces. | Success equations are explicit premises of enabledness results. Safety conditional on successful preparation does not assume every hook succeeds or has correct economic accounting. |
| `CRYPTO-BINDING` | Encodings are unambiguous and the hashes used by the claim bind the relevant inputs/domains. | Conditional mathematical results exist. Concrete cryptographic interpretation remains separate. |
| `CRYPTO-ID` | `IdAssumptions` in `Contracts.NativeTokenVault` and `Contracts.AssetRouter` is a theorem premise. It asserts that the asset-id encoding is injective on all 256-bit (chain, token) pairs or (chain, sender, data) triples, and never zero. | **Unsatisfiable**, proved: `Contracts.NativeTokenVault.IdAssumptionsUnsatisfiable`, `Contracts.AssetRouter.IdAssumptionsUnsatisfiable`. No function from pairs of 256-bit words to 256-bit words is injective, so every property taking this premise holds vacuously. That is eleven vault properties, including the `UnguardedInflowUnbacked` countermodel (only its `Valid` conjunct uses the premise), and the router's `NoHijack` and `NtvTouchesOnlyItsOwnIds`. `PerChainIsolationFails` no longer takes it. Each has a bounded restatement. Vault: the `…On` forms (`StepInPreservesValidOn` for `StepPreservesValid`), `RegisterNativeIdFreshOrCollision`, `SurplusMonotoneNoHash` and `LoggedRunSound`. Router, per call: `NoHijackOn`, `NtvTouchesOnlyItsOwnIdsOn` and the `…OrCollision` forms. Router, over runs: `HandlerRegistrationRuns` (`LoggedRunNoHijack`, `LoggedRunNtvTouchesOnlyItsOwnIds`) for the single writer, and for every writer `NoHijackOnExceptCounterpart` (pair-pinned), `L1RunWeakValidOn` (logged triples) and `L1CounterpartAtVaultIdOrLoggedClash` (logged clash). These assume injectivity only on the compared or logged inputs (`IdAssumptionsOn`, `IdInjOn`), as do the newer topics that hash ids (`AtomicRecovery`, `RegistrationUnified`, `LegacyFallback`). Reduction-style (collision-finding) forms with keccak as the id hash are in `DataEncoding` (`AssetIdRuns`, `AssetIdRecovery`): no premise on keccak, and the conclusion or a keccak collision (vault: or zero preimage) on 96-byte preimages the call or the logged run names. Restated so far: the router's `NoHijackOrCollision`, `NtvTouchesOnlyItsOwnIdsOrCollision`, `LoggedRunNoHijack` and `LoggedRunKeepsVaultRoutes`, the vault's `RegisterNativeIdFreshOrCollision` and `LoggedRunSound`, and `AtomicRecovery`'s `CustomAssetRecoveryRevertsOrCollision` and `CustomHandlerDepositRefundReverts`. For the others, an `IdInjOn enc S` or `IdAssumptionsOn e S` premise with `enc := routerEnc keccak` or `e := vaultEnc keccak` is exactly the absence of such an event among the preimages of `S` (`RouterIdInjOnIffNoCollision`, `VaultIdAssumptionsOnIffNoKeccakEvent`). |
| `PROTO-EVIDENCE` | Evidence authenticates the correct source/destination, commitment, settlement layer, history, deadline, and finality. A source chain supplies the funded preparation being authenticated. | Abstract interop results are conditional. An authenticated commitment alone does not establish honest execution on an arbitrary admitted chain. For the atomic path's source binding the premise is exactly that the chain whose tree vouches runs the pinned code (`Contracts.SourceBinding`, `MODEL-SOURCE-BINDING`); no other chain is assumed, and `WithoutOnlyInteropCenterSourceUnbound` shows it is load-bearing. |
| `PROTO-TX-STATUS` | `MessageRoot.proveL1ToL2TransactionStatusShared` accepts a query only for the L1 → L2 transaction's real L2 status: `∀ q, env.accepts q → truth q.chainId q.l2TxHash = some q.status` (`Contracts.L1Nullifier`). | Theorem premise of `ClearStatusIsTrue` and `NoFailureClearOfSucceededDeposit`; satisfiable by a prover that accepts exactly the real statuses (`LedgerRunWitness`, `SameKeyClearedTwiceWitness`); load-bearing (`SoundProverLoadBearing`). Rests on the L2 bootloader's status log, the log Merkle proof and settlement; not proved here. |
| `PROTO-TXHASH-FRESH` | The bridgehub's canonical L1 → L2 transaction hashes are fresh per chain, so a run records each `(chainId, txHash)` at most once. | Theorem premise of `L1Nullifier.AtMostOneClearPerDeposit` and `NestedAtMostOneClearPerDeposit` (as a count on the run's log). The nullifier's `DepositExists` only rejects an occupied slot, so the premise is load-bearing: a key recorded, cleared and recorded again is cleared twice (`TxHashFreshnessLoadBearing`; also `RepeatedKeyAccepted`). |
| `PROTO-L2-LOGS` | ZKsync OS side of an L1 status proof (`Contracts.MessageVerification`, `MODEL-STATUS-PROOF`): the batch root recorded on L1 is `ChainBatchRootTree.compute` over the `FullMerkle` fold of the batch's log leaves at some height, padded with `keccak(new bytes(88))` (`BatchRootCommitsLogs`); and every log of the batch whose sender is the bootloader is the status log of an L1 → L2 transaction carrying that transaction's real status (`BootloaderLogsAreStatusLogs`). | Theorem premises: `BatchRootCommitsLogs` of `LogProofOfAbsentLogLocatesBreak`, `StatusProofOfUnrealStatusLocatesBreak`, `FailureProofForSucceededTxLocatesBreak`, `FailureProofForSucceededTxIsKeccakBreak` and `HeldLogIsProvable`; `BootloaderLogsAreStatusLogs` of the three status results among them (`StatusProofOfUnrealStatusLocatesBreak`, `FailureProofForSucceededTxLocatesBreak`, `FailureProofForSucceededTxIsKeccakBreak`). Not checkable on L1: `proveL2LogInclusionShared` accepts a proof for any log the tree holds, whatever its sender. `BootloaderLogsAreStatusLogs` is load-bearing (`BootloaderPremiseLoadBearing`). Discharging either needs the bootloader's and the log hooks' semantics, which are not in this repository. |
| `OPS-CONFIG` | Actual initialization, upgrades, registry changes, pause/admin actions, and dependency selection stay within the verified transition system or preserve its invariants. | Most such transitions are outside the current trace. Fixed config is a model boundary, not a theorem about arbitrary upgrades. `SourceRegistrationInput.l1Nonzero` is one: on L2, `_l1ChainId()` is whatever `updateL2` stored, unchecked for zero. Pause and unpause of the L2 center, router and vault are modelled as owner steps (`MODEL-PAUSE`): the safety results hold over them verbatim, and a committed flow whose destination vault stays paused is neither executed nor refunded without a located break until the owner unpauses (`PermanentDestinationPauseStrandsLeg`). |
| `CRYPTO-MERKLE-BUNDLE` | `HashAssumptions` (`L2InteropCommitmentTree/Specs/TreeRoot.lean`): global node-pair injectivity, leaf-hash injectivity, leaf/node and padding domain separation. | **Unsatisfiable** — `HashAssumptionsUnsatisfiable` proves no `h`, `z0`, `hl` meet it. Every result taking it is vacuous until restated in collision form (see the table below). The countermodels `SubsetFlowPassesUncheckedGate`, `SelfOnlyGateAdmitsMixedOutcome` and `StaleRootRefundsDeliveredLeg` never used it and no longer take it. Separately, `padNotLeaf` is false at the pin (`PaddingCollisionRefundsDeliveredLeg`, which needs no premise). |
| `CRYPTO-PAIR-INJ` | Direct global-injectivity premises outside the bundles: a `hinj : ∀ a b c d, h a b = h c d → a = c ∧ b = d` argument (Merkle `Verifier`, `LastLeaf`'s accepted-path results, Timeout's last-batch results, and the pre-existing FullMerkle/Merkle results), `FlowHashInj` (`List FlowLeg → ℕ → UInt256` injective) and `CommitValueInj` (pair-injective commit value). | **Unsatisfiable**, proved: `Contracts.InteropCommitmentTree.not_nodeInj`, `Contracts.Atomicity.FlowHashInjUnsatisfiable`, `Contracts.Refund.CommitValueInjUnsatisfiable`. `CrossFlowRefundYieldsCollision`, `SharedCommitmentYieldsCollision` and `LastLeafOrBreak` take none of these, but their collision disjuncts are unpinned and therefore always true (`UnpinnedCollisionsAlwaysExist`, `HashBreakAlways`); `SharedCommitmentYieldsCollision` has a run-level restatement with content (`SharedCommitment`: `SharedCommitmentCoexistsIffCrossChain`, `ChainLogIffAppendable`). `distinct_sends_distinct_hashes` (`InteropDataEncoding` `BundleHashEncoding`) takes global injectivity of `saltHash` and `bundleHashOf`; at `Hash = UInt256` both are **unsatisfiable** (`SaltHashInjUnsatisfiable`, `BundleHashInjUnsatisfiable`), and `SaltHashNotInjOnPackedPreimages` shows injectivity fails even on the well-formed `abi.encodePacked(address, bytes32)` preimages. Pinned replacements: every Merkle/FullMerkle result taking `hinj` in `Merkle/Properties/RootForgeryPinned.lean`; the bundle hash in `BundleHashPinned`. |
| `PROTO-DA` | `DataAvailable`: the provers can obtain the source-chain IMT paths the gate needs (not the destination's settlement proof or imported root: `PROTO-ROOT-IMPORT`). | Theorem premise of the none-or-all results. The countermodel `WithoutDaCommittedLegIsStuck` uses `HashAssumptions` and is vacuous; its restatement `WithoutDaCommittedLegIsStuckOrBreak` takes no hash premise (repaired-forms table). Also a premise of `MissedDeadlineRefundableByHonestProof` and `AcceptedGateCompleteByHonestProofs` (`RefundStrong`), for presenting the honest IMT absence proof (on the BEGIN branch, and on the END branch for the latter); the other `RefundStrong` completeness results (`RefundableIffMissedDeadline`, `AcceptedGateCompleteFromAnyPostDeadlineRoot`, `AcceptedGateIffMissedDeadline`, `EndBranchAcceptsHonestProofs`, `StrandedFlowCommittedLegRefundable`) say that an accepted proof exists and take no `Access`. Not dischargeable by source correspondence. |
| `PROTO-ROOT-IMPORT` | `ImportAvailable` and `SettlementDataAvailable` (`Contracts.RootImport`, `Protocol/InteropAtomicity/Specs/RootImport.lean`, `c67894b9`): the destination has imported settlement-layer roots covering every batch settled by the deadline (`L2InteropRootStorage` imports are bootloader-only, `:58-76`), and the multi-hop settlement proof `_authenticateRoot` needs is presentable. | Theorem premises of the destination-indexed finalizability results, none of which takes a hash premise. Each takes `SettlementDataAvailable`. `ImportAvailable` is a premise of `ExecutedImpliesAllFinalizableAtOrBreak`, `ExecutedAtImpliesAllFinalizableAtOrBreak`, `NoneOrAllAtOrBreak`, `NoneOrAllAtOrBreakPinned`, `NoneOrAllAtOrBreakPinnedFromRuns` and `ExecutedImpliesRealFlowFinalizableAtOrLoggedClash`; `MemberIsFinalizableAt` takes `RootImported` for its one batch instead, and `ExecutedImpliesFinalityPersistsAtOrBreak` takes no import premise (its conclusion is conditional on `RootImported`). Load-bearing: `WithoutImportCrossDestinationFails` (the negation of `ExecutedAtImpliesAllFinalizableAtOrBreak` with the import premise on `dB` dropped), via `WithoutImportFlowSitsHalfExecuted` (one destination executes a leg on break-free evidence, a sibling's destination, which has imported nothing, cannot pass, and no leg is refundable without a located `AbsenceBreak` on the refund's absence proof, while `FlowFinalizableBy` holds; what blocks the refunds is the legs' on-time commitment, not the missing import), and `WithoutImportLoggedFormFails` (the same for `ExecutedImpliesRealFlowFinalizableAtOrLoggedClash`). The coverage half is load-bearing too: `WithoutCoverageCrossDestinationFails` (the import premise on `dB` weakened to "`dB` has imported some root of `sl`"), via `StaleImportFlowSitsHalfExecuted` (`dB` has imported a genuine root that does not cover the legs' batch; no leg is finalizable on `dB` under honest IMT access, and every execution on `dB` carries a `GateBreak`). `SettlementDataAvailable` is load-bearing by `WithoutSettlementDataLegIsStuck`. `ImportAvailable` is discharged by one imported root created at or after the deadline plus `PROTO-AGGREGATES` (`ImportAvailableOfPostDeadlineImport`). Not dischargeable by source correspondence: an import is a destination-local transition, and source DA does not supply it. Model boundary: aggregation paths inside a settlement proof are idealized as honest (soundness direction only). Source chains are assumed to settle directly on `sl`, so the one-hop settlement proof applies; Gateway-settled sources are out of scope because `_proveL2LeafInclusionRecursive` allows one recursion level (`DepthMoreThanOneForRecursiveMerkleProof`, `L2MessageVerification.sol:34-36`), and for them `SettlementDataAvailable` is optimistic. |
| `PROTO-AGGREGATES` | `Aggregates`: an imported settlement-layer root aggregates every batch that settled before it. Per chain `c` it implies `StaleSnapshotBranches.LaterBatchesLate (R.upTo c)` at the root's last batch `R.upTo c` of that chain: every batch numbered after it settles no earlier than the root. The end branch needs that at its proven batch `B`, so `B` must be the root's last batch of the chain; the repository's compositions read that off the last-leaf check under `PROTO-CONTIGUOUS-PUSH`. | Premise of the derived timeout decision; load-bearing by `StaleRootRefundsDeliveredLeg`, which takes no hash premise. Also a premise of the bridge-level END-branch composition (`Applications.BurnMintBridge.EndBranch`: `EndViaNeverFinalized`, `CheckedBridgeNoDoublePayment`, `AcceptedEndOnTimeLocatesBreak`), load-bearing there by `StaleRootDoublePays`; the BEGIN branch (`BeginViaNeverFinalized`) and completeness (`TimeoutComplete`) do not use it. That composition uses the per-chain step: `outsideRootLate_of_aggregates` (`Examples/BurnMintBridge/Proofs/EndBranch.lean:225-234`) turns `RootAggregates` into `LastBatchInRoot.OutsideRootLate (· < R.upTo c + 1)`, which is equivalent to `LaterBatchesLate (R.upTo c)`. Also a premise of `Contracts.RootImport.ImportAvailableOfPostDeadlineImport`, and, in its per-chain form `LaterBatchesLate`, of `StaleSnapshotBranches.EndBranchGivesPin` and `EndBranchNotRedeemable`, load-bearing there by `EndBranchNeedsLaterBatchesLate`. Two names are easily confused: `StaleSnapshotBranches.OutsideRootLate` with true root membership (every batch not in the root settles no earlier than the root) is a premise of no headline in that topic, while `LastBatchInRoot.OutsideRootLate (· < nBatches)`, which reads the root as holding batches `0 .. nBatches - 1`, is the `hout` the base `end_branch_from_onchain_check` takes. In the operational model (`MODEL-INTEROP-OPERATIONAL`) the roots are the run's own, and the strict form `AggregatesBefore` (every batch that settled strictly before the root) is a theorem of every run (`OpRunRootsAggregateBefore`); it suffices for the END branch (`IsLastOnTimeDerivedBefore`). The non-strict form can fail for a root recorded before a same-timestamp settlement (`OpRootMayMissSameTimeBatch`), so results taking `Aggregates` apply to operational roots only through their re-proved forms (`OpRefundedLegMissedDeadlineOrBreak`, `OpRefundStaysDeserved`). What stays assumed there is the settlement-layer behaviour the steps encode: in-order settlement, a non-decreasing clock, and a recorded root holding every batch settled so far. In `RefundStrong`, `AcceptedGateIffMissedDeadline` takes the strict form `AggregatesBefore` (`Protocol/InteropAtomicity/Specs/Operational.lean`: a batch settled strictly before the root was created is inside it), which `Aggregates` implies and every recorded root of an operational run satisfies (`OpRunRootsAggregateBefore`). Only its soundness half uses it; its completeness half is `AcceptedGateCompleteFromAnyPostDeadlineRoot`, which, like `AcceptedGateCompleteByHonestProofs` and `EndBranchAcceptsHonestProofs`, takes no aggregation premise. `Contracts.BatchPosition` takes the strict form `AggregatesBefore` (a batch that settled strictly before the root was created is in it; implied by `Aggregates`, `AggregatesImpliesBefore`, and strictly weaker, `AggregatesBeforeIsWeaker`), or derives it (`AggregatesBeforeFromPushLog`) from `AggregatesPushed` (a batch pushed strictly before the root was created is among its entries), `ClockFromPushes` and `GapsAfterRootLate` (a batch after the root's last entry that never reached this layer is not given a time strictly before the root), the last load-bearing for the model's composition by `MigrationGapIsLoadBearing`. Discharged in its strict form (`Contracts.InteropOperational.AggregatesBefore`, which is what the END branch uses) for a system whose batches are a `MessageRootBase` run's pushed positions, clocked by their `l1Timestamp`, and for every historical root of that run: `Contracts.SettlementRoots.HistoricalRootAggregatesBefore`; the `…FromMessageRoot` forms of the timeout and refund headlines take no aggregation premise. The non-strict form holds under strictly increasing timestamps for the roots as they stand (`HistoricalRootAggregatesStrictTs`) and fails for a root read mid-block or across equal timestamps (`Contracts.MessageRoot.Aggregation.Properties.MidBlockReadEscapesRoot`, `EqualTimestampEscapesRoot`). That discharge rests on `MODEL-MESSAGE-ROOT`, on Executor-checked imports (`MODEL-ROOT-STORAGE`), and on reading the system's trees as the commitment trees at the end of each pushed batch. The other consumers listed above (`Applications.BurnMintBridge.EndBranch`, `StaleSnapshotBranches`' `LaterBatchesLate` results, `Contracts.RootImport.ImportAvailableOfPostDeadlineImport`) are not re-derived from it and still take the non-strict, batch-number-indexed premise. |
| `PROTO-BATCH-SNAPSHOT` | `BeginIsPrevEnd`: the IMT state at the begin of batch `n+1` is the state at the end of batch `n` (`AttackVectors.StaleSnapshotBranches.BeginIsPrevEnd`, over abstract leaf sets, and `AttackVectors.TimeoutSoundness.BeginIsPrevEnd`, over value sets). This is how c67894b9 documents the bootloader's snapshots: chain-batch-root leaf 2 is the IMT root before the batch's first block and leaf 3 the root after its last (`ChainBatchRootTree.sol:13-14`), both read from the tree's storage at every batch boundary (`ChainBatchRootTree.sol:24-25`, `L2InteropCommitmentTree.sol:15-17`), and `begin(N) == end(N-1)` (`AtomicInteropProof.sol:68-70`). | Premise of `StaleSnapshotBranches.BeginBranchGivesPin` and `BeginBranchNotRedeemable`, and of `TimeoutSoundness.begin_absence_of_beginIsPrevEnd`. As `TimeoutSoundness` already records, no on-chain check establishes it: `_authenticateRoot` authenticates one IMT-root leaf, 2 or 3, of one batch and never relates two batches. Load-bearing by `StaleSnapshotBranches.BeginBranchNeedsBeginIsPrevEnd` and `TimeoutSoundness.begin_branch_needs_beginIsPrevEnd`, which take no hash premise. The end-branch bridge `LaterBatchesLate` is listed under `PROTO-AGGREGATES`. `RefundStrong`'s completeness results take the BEGIN branch's tree from `Contracts.Atomicity.beginTree`, which defines the BEGIN tree of batch `n+1` as the END tree of batch `n`, so their BEGIN cases rest on it. |
| `PROTO-CONTIGUOUS-PUSH` | `AtomicInteropProof/Specs/LastBatchInRootOffset.lean`: after a chain's first pushed batch `f`, every later batch is pushed into its chain tree, so leaf position `p` is batch `batchAt f p = f + p`. At c67894b9 every atomic-interop source chain has `f = 0` on L1 (a ZKsync OS genesis chain, registered by `createNewChain` with `addNewChain(_chainId, 0)` and `seedGenesisRoot`). | Premise (as `B = batchAt f p`) of `EndBranchFromOnchainCheckOffset`; implicit, with `f = 0`, wherever a chain-tree leaf position is read as a batch number: `LastBatchInRoot.end_branch_from_onchain_check`, `Contracts.Timeout.RootBacking` and the END-branch compositions built on them; `RefundStrong` also reads `N ≤ R.upTo c` as "batch `N` lies inside `R`" on the BEGIN branch. `MessageRootBase` (c67894b9) does not enforce it: `_recordChainBatchRoot` makes batch NUMBERS consecutive, but `setMigratingChainBatchNumber` can advance the counter past batches never pushed here, which on L1 happens when a chain returns from Gateway (`ChainAssetHandlerBase.sol:386`); that Gateway round trip is the realistic breaker at the pin. The record-only `addChainBatchRoot` passes the same `addChainBatchRootRestriction` as `addChainBatchRootV32`, but no executor at the pin calls it (`Executor.sol:154` calls only `addChainBatchRootV32`), so that route needs an executor facet downgrade. `BatchNumberBoundOrCollision` states, as a case split, that the proof's batch number equals the stored leaf's or the two named preimages collide; it says nothing about the stored leaf's number versus its position. `Contracts.BatchPosition` (`Protocol/InteropAtomicity/Specs/BatchPosition.lean`) does not take this assumption: its chain-tree entries carry their own batch numbers, and its END-branch results read the batch number from the submitted leaf preimage, and in its place they carry a located `LeafPreimageClash` on two named preimages (or take the bounded `BatchLeafInjOn`) and take `AggregatesBefore`. `RoundTripBreaksContiguity` shows the breaker reachable in its `ChainTreeStep` model of `MessageRootBase`: a genesis-seeded chain's tree after a Gateway round trip holds batches `0, 1, 6`, which no first-batch offset makes contiguous; `RoundTripTimeoutWitness` composes on that tree. |
| `PROTO-CAH-DELIVERY` | Migration messages of `Contracts.ChainAssetHandler`: an L1 → settlement-layer migration transaction executes once on its destination, with success (its `bridgeMint` ran) or failure, and the status proof L1's nullifier checks is truthful; `L1Nullifier._verifyAndClearTransfer` consumes each deposit's status once (`bridge/L1Nullifier.sol:218-248`); an L2 → L1 migration message is finalized at most once and does not fail permanently (the source's own assumption, `Migrator.sol:200-202`). | Built into the model's `MintOk` (a pending message, minted at its destination), `ConfirmOk` (a proved status, consumed once) and the `failDeposit` step; not discharged here. The enabledness results (`GatewayAcceptsArrival`, `L1AcceptsReturn`, `FailedDepositConfirmable`) say when a delivery step is possible; that it is taken is `OPS-PROGRESS`. |
| `PROTO-PRIORITY-ROOTS` | `RootsAreLoggedPrefixRoots h z roots Q log` (`PriorityTree/Specs/SegmentBinding.lean`, `b17415d20`): every recorded `historicalRoots` entry is `bytes32(0)` (what `PriorityTree.setup` records, `PriorityTree.sol:59-63`) or the root `rootOf h z (Q.take m) H` of a logged prefix `(m, H)` of the pushed hashes `Q`, `m ≤ Q.length`, padding `z`. Separately, the item-level premise of `ProcessBatchQueueSegmentOrBreak` and `ProcessedRunIsQueueSegmentOrBreak`: no processed item equals `z` (`ZERO_LEAF_HASH = keccak256("")`). | Premise of `ProcessBatchSegmentOrBreak`, `ProcessBatchQueueSegmentOrBreak` and `ProcessedRunIsQueueSegmentOrBreak`; satisfiable (`SegmentBindingWitness`). On a run of `setup` and `push` it follows from `DynamicIncrementalMerkle.push` returning `rootOf` at the least height that holds the leaves, which this package does not prove (stated without a finished proof on the unmerged `origin/cov2/merkle-libs`, `PushRunHeightIsMinimal`). Roots recorded by `initFromCommitment` (`PriorityTree.sol:116-132`, `Migrator.sol:289`, `:291`) are not covered. The item-level premise is load-bearing (`PaddingItemsAdvancePastQueue`) and is not discharged here: `processBatch`'s NatSpec (`PriorityTree.sol:77-82`) leaves item validity to the proof that binds the rolling hash. |
| `PROTO-PROG-DA` | The ZKsync OS program's `daCommitment`, which the proof authenticates through the batch output hash (`Committer.sol:291-311`). Under `BLOBS_AND_PUBDATA_KECCAK256` the results take it to be `keccak (l2Output sd (keccak pub) lin)` for the batch's pubdata `pub`; `Contracts.ZKsyncOSCommitterDA.RollupPubdataPublishedOrCollision` adds `pub ≠ [] → lin ≠ []`. Under `BLOBS_ZKSYNC_OS`, `BlobsCommittedHashesFreshOrCollision` takes it to be `keccak` of the concatenated versioned hashes of the batch's blobs. | Off-chain; not modelled or discharged here. Without `pub ≠ [] → lin ≠ []` the rollup headline is false (`RollupProgramPremiseLoadBearing`); `RelayedPubdataRelayedOrCollision` needs only the commitment's form. |
| `CRYPTO-CBR-DOCUMENTED` | `ReservedSubtreeDocumented` (`RESERVED_SUBTREE_NODE = keccak(z1 ‖ z1)` with `z1 = keccak(0 ‖ 0)`) and `EmptyImtRootDocumented` (`EMPTY_IMT_ROOT` is keccak of 96 zero bytes), `Contracts.ChainBatchRootTree`. | Theorem premises on two, resp. one, specific keccak inputs, never injectivity; satisfiable (`ComputeRootNeedsDocumentedValue`, the witness keccaks). Pinned by unit tests at `b17415d20` (`ChainBatchRootTree.t.sol:15-21, 58-61`) and recomputed off chain with `cast keccak`; Lean does not evaluate keccak. Results that need neither (`LiveLeafOpeningPinsLeafOrCollision`, `ImtRootHopPinsLeafOrCollision`, `LogProofPassesLeafZeroOrBreak`, `GenesisSeed` except `GenesisImtLeavesOpenSetupRoot`) do not take them. |
| `PROTO-SHARED-ROOT` | `SharedTreeBacking` and `ChainTreeEntries` (`AtomicInteropProof/Specs/AuthenticateRootAtV35.lean`, `b17415d20`): the imported settlement-layer root `interopRoots(slChainId, slBlock).root` is the `rootOf` of the settlement layer's shared tree at that block, with padding `SHARED_ROOT_TREE_EMPTY_HASH`, and each occupied slot `i` holds `chainIdLeafHash(chainRoot i, chainAt i)`; every slot registered to the source chain was last written with the root of the source chain's tree; and each entry of that tree is `batchLeafHash(chainBatchRoot, batchNumber, timestamp)` with `chainBatchRoot` the height-3 `ChainBatchRootTree` over its leaves (zero-padded). On the settlement layer `MessageRootBase` pushes exactly such entries and writes such slots (`MessageRootBase.sol:201-214`, `:287`) and records the shared tree's root per block (`:223-224`); the chain batch root's shape is the zksync-os bootloader's (`ChainBatchRootTree.sol:6-8`). | Premise of `EndBranchTwoHopLastLeafOrBreakV35` (without `ChainTreeEntries`), `AuthenticateRootSoundV35` and `EndBranchSoundV35`, met together with all their other premises by `EndBranchV35Satisfiable`. No check on the importing chain establishes it when the root is used: the bootloader imports roots, and the executor compares each imported `(root, timestamp)` with the settlement layer's `historicalRoot` only when the importing batch executes (`Executor.sol:96-97`, `:117-158`). That `FullMerkle`'s stored root is `rootOf` is `FullMerkle` `CachedNodes`; that a chain tree's root is `rootOf` of its leaves at its height is the convention of `LastLeafFaithful` and `Contracts.Timeout.RootBacking`. A chain added and not yet pushed holds `chainIdLeafHash(0, c)`, so the source-slot premise fails for it until its first push. Entry `i` is not assumed to be batch `i` (`PROTO-CONTIGUOUS-PUSH` is not used): the results bind the proof's batch number to the entry's own. |
| `OPS-PROGRESS` | Required gas/resources, usable dependencies, submissions, inclusion, and chain progress are available. | Needed for deliverability/eventual completion, not silently assumed by a safety proof conditional on success. |

Some historical Merkle results assume *global* pair-injectivity of a
`UInt256 × UInt256 → UInt256` hash. That premise cannot hold for a concrete
256-bit hash on its full domain. A checked implication with that premise is not
by itself a concrete security guarantee. Prefer the existing restricted-pair
results where applicable, and ultimately state a collision-finding reduction or
an explicitly bounded no-collision premise for the values the proof compares.
For the Merkle and FullMerkle results the pinned forms now exist (`RootForgeryPinned`).
Do not relabel global injectivity as a proved property of Keccak. The ported
vault and router models carry the same kind of premise: see `CRYPTO-ID`.

### Results whose premises cannot hold

Each premise below is proved unsatisfiable (`CRYPTO-MERKLE-BUNDLE`, `CRYPTO-ID`,
`CRYPTO-PAIR-INJ`), so these properties are vacuously true: they record intended
claims and proof structure, not guarantees. The list was computed from the property
statements; `global-hinj` means an unrestricted `∀ a b c d, h a b = h c d → a = c ∧ b = d`
argument (the `_on` variants restricted to a predicate `P` are satisfiable and not listed).
The FullMerkle and Merkle rows predate the consolidation. Every one of them now has a pinned
replacement with no hash premise, mapped one-to-one in the header of
`EraSpec/Contracts/Merkle/Properties/RootForgeryPinned.lean`; four use existing
`PathCollision` results (`walk_pins_leaf_and_sibs`, `AcceptPinsEntry`, `AcceptForcesHeight`,
`AcceptPinsEntryAnyLength`).

A collision conclusion helps only if it is **pinned**: its colliding inputs must be
determined by the instance. "…or some collision exists" is always true, because every
map into `UInt256` from a larger domain has collisions (`exists_pair_collision`). So
`LastLeafOrBreak` (`HashBreakAlways`), `CrossFlowRefundYieldsCollision` and
`SharedCommitmentYieldsCollision` (`UnpinnedCollisionsAlwaysExist`) are trivially true
and carry no information either. Pinned results so far: `WalkPinsOrCollision` and
`AcceptPinsEntryOrCollision` (`Merkle/Properties/PathCollision.lean`; the collision is
at a named level of the submitted path, and `HonestWalkNoCollision` shows the
predicate is not a tautology), `CrossFlowIdClashIsPinnedCollision` (a collision on
the two flows' own preimages), and the tree-versus-tree (`TreeCollision`), walk-versus-walk
(`WalkCollision`) and level (`LevelCollision`) extractors of `MerkleSpec.Pinned`
(`Merkle/Properties/RootForgeryPinned.lean`), each on the instance's own pairs and false on
equal instances for every hash (`EqualInstancesNeverCollide`). Restated in these forms:
the Merkle/FullMerkle rows (`RootForgeryPinned`), `ExecutedImpliesRealFlowFinalizable`
(`ExecutedImpliesRealFlowFinalizableOrLoggedClash`), `CommitmentPinsLeg`
(`CommitmentPinsLoggedLeg`) and `distinct_sends_distinct_hashes` (`BundleHashPinned`).
`SharedCommitmentYieldsCollision` is restated at run level, where the run decides it
(`SharedCommitment`, below).
Restating any remaining row in pinned form, or with a bounded no-collision premise on
instance-determined inputs, is the open repair.

A related narrowness, not vacuity: router results over runs from fresh storage (`RunValid`
and the like) concern vault-only runs, since in the single-writer model no non-vault call
is enabled from empty storage (`OnlyVaultActsFromEmpty`). `HandlerRegistrationRuns`
therefore starts from any router.

### Repaired forms that stand

Restatements with no unsatisfiable premise. Each was checked by an adversarial
reviewer for vacuity (premises satisfiable) and triviality (the collision disjunct is
pinned to the instance and false on a named instance). The vacuous originals above
are unchanged, for comparison.
