Recorded but without protocol content: `FlowIdCheckPinsLegListOrCollision`,
`SubsetFlowCheckedOnlyByCollision`, `CommitmentPinsLegOrCollision` (excluded middle)
and the bounded `FlowIdCheckPinsLegListOn` and `SubsetFlowRejectedByCheckOn` (premise is
the conclusion contraposed). The flow-id form with protocol content is
`ExecutedImpliesRealFlowFinalizableOrLoggedClash` (`FlowBinding`): its clash disjunct ranges
only over flows the run's own logged calls presented, and
`SubsetFlowAttackLandsInLoggedClash` shows the truncated-flow attack lands only there.
`CommitmentPinsLegOn` now has protocol content on a run's own append log:
`RunRefutesLoggedCommitClash` (`OncePerLeg`) discharges its premise there, giving
`CommitmentPinsLoggedLeg`. `AttackVectors.RecoveryLimits.validation_unbypassable` concludes
its own premise `sole_path`; for the center's appended bundles that premise is discharged
by `Contracts.InteropCenter.SolePath` and instantiated in `ValidationUnbypassableDischarged`.
`AttackVectors.AtomicSourceBinding.Properties.atomic_source_bound` concludes what its premise
`HonestCommits` states; `Contracts.SourceBinding.AtomicSourceBoundOrBreak` and
`AtomicSourceBoundOn` derive it from the vouching chain's code.

Residual weak points, reviewed: `NoneOrAllOrBreak`'s `GateBreak` does not say which
leg (the per-leg results do); `RefundBreak` does not say which logged call when several
marked the obligation; the firing witnesses use deliberately weak toy hashes and live on
small trees.

Source findings tied to results above. Each was checked against the pinned source by two
independent verifiers, who agreed on the classification given. Each is reported to the
maintainer of this repo, not yet confirmed upstream.

- Classified as a bug: `L2AssetRouter.recoverAtomicCall` always calls the vault, whatever
  the asset's handler, so recovery of a custom-handler asset reverts and strands the whole
  atomic bundle (`RecoveryIgnoresHandler`, `CustomHandlerBundleIsStranded`); a direct
  atomic call to the router can be refunded as a recovery and mint unbacked
  (`DirectCallMintsUnbacked`); `calculateRoot` accepts the honest siblings of an empty slot
  against the stored root (`CThreeLeafWitness`), and, against `FullMerkle.sol`'s zero-value
  warning, a padding slot is indistinguishable from a leaf at the published root
  (`PublishedRootPaddingHitFires`); a zero receiver is accepted at the source
  burn and rejected at every destination mint (`ZeroReceiverOnlyRecoverable`);
  `replaceUpgradeTransition` does not check the replacement's `oldProtocolVersion()`, and
  a misreporting slot can never be replaced again (`MisreportingSlotIsFrozen`); the CTM
  does not disable `renounceOwnership` (`RenouncedAuthorityIsStranded`).
- Likely a bug, needs deployment context: an unmigrated legacy bridged token can be
  registered as native by anyone (`UnregisteredLegacyTokenRegistersNative`).
- NatSpec or documentation wrong: "upgrade at any time" in `ICTMUpgradeExecutor`
  (`NotAtAnyTime`); `requireReleaseRouting`'s stated precondition (`EmptySelectorRowFoolsCountCheck`,
  `CorruptStorageFoolsCountCheck`); what `applyRows`' verification pass catches
  (`DuplicateRowHidesReinitMove`, `ReinitLeakMovesNamedProxyOffEdge`); the asset-id check
  claimed for `bridgeMint` on the registered branch (`BridgeMintIgnoresUnreadFields`);
  custom deployment trackers, which on L2 only the vault can be (`L2OnlyVaultRegisters`).

## Current bridge claims and their dependencies

These rows distinguish what is proved *inside the model* from the additional
work needed to apply it to a deployed artifact. They are a manual register;
the axiom checker does not infer or validate this mapping.

| Claim | Visible premises and model scope | Additional concrete connection |
|---|---|---|
| Token `burn_exact` | Successful modeled burn plus `WellFormed`; ledger bounds and exact debit/supply effect are derived. | `MODEL-LEDGER`, `REP-TOKEN`, relevant `VM-*`, `SOL-*`, and `BUILD-*`. |
| Vault `registration_establishes_representation` / `registration_establishes_ledger` | Successful modeled registration; no prior ledger/registry agreement premise. Uses `MODEL-FRESH` and the projected install sequence. | `REP-INIT`, `VM-STORAGE/CALL/DEPLOY`, `SOL-CONTROL/ABI`, dependency frames, and build identity/preservation. |
| Vault `preparation_backed` | `WellFormed` and successful modeled preparation. Universally quantified over the hook functions of the stated type. | `REP-TOKEN`, `REP-VAULT`, `DEP-FRAME`, ledger representation, call/ABI/arithmetic/build connections. |
| Vault `failed_preparation_atomic` | `prepare = none`; result follows from the definition of `transact`. | `MODEL-COMMIT`, `VM-REVERT`, `SOL-CONTROL`, and actual call/frame coverage. |
| Vault `preparation_enabled` | Named guards, sufficient balance, valid identity/origin, and explicit successful account/metadata equations. | The same representation/call connections plus `DEP-RESULTS` and execution resources. It does not prove eventual submission. |
| `Codegen.BridgedTokenSlice.mint_refines` / `burn_refines` / `transfer_refines` | `WellFormed` model state, `Rel` between interpreter storage and it, ABI-valid arguments. Both directions: model rejection iff revert; acceptance gives a related state, exact logs and return value. | `GEN-EMIT` for the generated contract; then `BUILD-*`, `VM-*`. Says nothing about the original contract (`REP-TOKEN` stays open; see `solidity/README.md` for test evidence). |
| `Codegen.BridgedTokenSlice.reachable_ledger` | A nonzero-vault deployment; any sequence of successful ABI-valid calls. Transferred from the model by `safety_transfer`. | As above. |
| `initialized_preparation_trace` | Successful registration, a local token-operation funding phase, and the closed preparation/spending trace. Derives both invariants and supply-plus-pending conservation. | All participating representation obligations, `MODEL-TRACE`, `REP-PUBLISH`, and `OPS-CONFIG`. Funding authorization/backing for the initial mint phase remains separate. |
| `Contracts.NativeTokenVault.Solvency` / `RunValid` / `NoInflation` | Any run of the registry-and-escrow model from fresh storage, with `IdAssumptions` and a nonzero chain id. | Vacuous as stated (`CRYPTO-ID`). Otherwise `MODEL-ESCROW`, registry and ledger representation, and `OPS-CONFIG`. Separate from the `NativeTokenVaultBase` preparation model. |
| `Contracts.AssetRouter.NoHijack` | One modelled `_setAssetHandlerAddressThisChain` call by a non-vault caller, with `IdAssumptions`. | Vacuous as stated (`CRYPTO-ID`). `OnlyTrackerOrNtvWrites`, `FreshIdNeedsNtv`, and `RunValid` do not use the premise. `RunValid` holds only for the registration-only fragment (`SingleWriterIsRegisterOnlyFragment`); with every writer it is false (`AllWritersRunValidFalse`), and the deployed-router invariant is `AllWriters.WeakValid` (`RunWeakValid`, `L1RunWeakValidOn`). Router storage and call correspondence are open. |
| `Contracts.AssetRouter.LoggedRouteIsLastOwnWrite` and the run-level router results | Logged runs of the single writer `_setAssetHandlerAddressThisChain` from an arbitrary router, with `IdInjOn` on the run's own triples plus the watched one. | Not vacuous (`…Satisfiable`). From fresh storage only the vault acts (`OnlyVaultActsFromEmpty`), so instances with a custom tracker need a start state left by L1's owner-only `setAssetDeploymentTracker`. The other writers are in `MODEL-ROUTER-WRITERS`. Router storage and call correspondence are open. |
| `Contracts.AssetRouter.AtomicRecovery.CustomHandlerBundleNeverRefunded` / `PreparedBurnRefundsDepositor` | Every registry state the registry model reaches; pinned `IdInjOn`; other targets leave the custom id's registry entry alone. The positive form needs a vault that knows the asset, unpaused, with the tracker hook returning. It is stated on `routerCall`, which is the router-produced call only for a destination other than L1; for an L1 destination the call targets `L1_ASSET_ROUTER` and its refund does not reach the router (`AtomicRecoveryFaithful`: `PreparedBurnRefundByDestination`, `PreparedBurnRefundFailsToL1`). | `MODEL-ATOMIC-RECOVERY`, `MODEL-ATOMIC-RECOVERY-DEST`, `SOL-ABI`, `DEP-FRAME`, `VM-REVERT`/`SOL-CONTROL` (no try/catch in `_recoverBundle`), `REP-PUBLISH`. The direct-call countermodel shows recovery is not bound to a source burn. |
| `Contracts.AssetRouter.AtomicRecoveryAtHead.RouterLegReachesBurnHandler` / `DirectBundleClaimIsNoOpAtHead` / `RevertingCallBlocksClaimAtHead` | At `b17415d20`. Headline (b): a run of the router-map writers from the post-burn maps that rotates nothing (or, in the logged form, `IdInjOn` on the asset's triple paired with each logged triple), a non-L1 destination and a nonzero vault address; any handler code, hooks and world. Headline (a): no call from the router and none with value. Headline (d): an invariant kept by the bundle's returning calls, under which one call always reverts. The rotation premise is load-bearing (`RotationToNoOpHandlerStrandsBurn`, `RotationToRevertingHandlerBlocksClaim`; upstream known issue 5). | `MODEL-ATOMIC-RECOVERY-HEAD`, `SOL-ABI`, `DEP-FRAME`, `VM-REVERT`/`SOL-CONTROL` (no try/catch in `_recoverBundle`), `OPS-CONFIG`. How the leg became `Revertable` is outside this model (`InteropAtomicity`). |
| Vault `BridgeMintExact` / `RecoverExact` / `FirstBridgeMintExact` | Successful modelled call on the registered bridged branch (or first bridge, with the asset id checked against the data's origin token). Exact credit and pre-mint hook are derived; no invariant premise. | `REP-RESOLVE`, `REP-TOKEN`, `DEP-FRAME`, `SOL-ABI`, `VM-CALL`, `MODEL-COMMIT`/`VM-REVERT`; first bridge also `REP-INIT`, `VM-DEPLOY`. |
| Vault `PrepareThenRecoverRoundTrip` / `RecoverAfterSpending` / `ResolutionIsForeignBridgeIn` | `WellFormed`, successful preparation, and `HooksCancel` (round trip) or hook success (spending; transfers only between the steps); `Corr` for the registry correspondence. `RoundTripNeedsCancellingHooks` shows the hook law is needed. | `REP-RESOLVE`, `REP-VAULT`, router recovery-call and tracker correspondence, `OPS-CONFIG` (pause and other transitions between the steps). At-most-once resolution is the callers' job (`VaultHasNoReplayGuard`). |
| `AssetBridgePreparation` `TwoChainConservation` / `TwoChainResolvesOnce` / `TwoChainExclusive` | Any two-chain run of preparations, spending on either chain, deliveries and refunds, from a `WellFormed` source (conservation) with both records empty, under `Sound` evidence. `Sound` is a premise; the instances below supply it, or a located break. | `MODEL-RESOLVE`, `MODEL-TRACE`, `REP-PUBLISH`, `REP-VAULT`, `REP-RESOLVE`, `PROTO-EVIDENCE`, `OPS-CONFIG`. |
| `InteropTwoChainConservedOrBreak` / `InteropTwoChainResolvesOnceOrBreak` | The same run with the atomic-interop evidence: delivery on `ExecutedViaBy` gate evidence over a `Wf` fixed history, refund on a logged `ReachLog` manager run. No hash premise; the books balance or a `RefundBreak` is located on the refund's own log. | As above, plus the fixed-history evidence model (the operational model, `MODEL-INTEROP-OPERATIONAL`, is not composed with this trace), the leg routing `route` (`REP-PUBLISH`), and O7–O9. |
| `MultichainTwoChainConserved` | Every commitment tree `Valid`, one snapshot, the bound gate with a genuine-leaf absence witness (`AbsenceWitnessAt`). | As above. At `c67894b9` the checked `verifyNonInclusion` also accepts the padding sentinel at an empty slot, which this gate does not cover (Correction (StaleSnapshotFaithful) in `ResolutionEvidence`). |
| `TwoChainRefundAvailable` | A fresh trace on a `WellFormed` source; a passing refund witness and a returning source hook. Enabledness in the model. | `DEP-RESULTS`, the omitted reject-only checks of `MODEL-RESOLVE`, `OPS-PROGRESS`. |
| `AssetBridgePreparation.FullRecovery.PreparedBundleFullyRecovered` | A preparation trace from a `WellFormed` vault with nothing pending; a bundle of router `finalizeDeposit` calls carrying a nonempty sub-multiset of the pending obligations; a manager world whose registry is `Corr`-related to the trace's vault, whose ledger of the tracked token is the trace's token, with the vault unpaused; a `Revertable` leg; a returning tracker run; the local token model. Supply headroom is derived. Balanced flows: a nested run whose steps on an already registered asset with a token are bridge-outs toward non-L1 chains and claim calls with equal totals, the vault as handler if a router call names the asset, and for the base asset a non-native origin. Ledger: any run or nested run under any checks; no L1 recovery needs only the flow manager's check. Enabledness (`RecoveriesAfterBurnSucceed`) needs a non-L1 destination and, for the base asset, a non-native origin. Read the authoritative statements in
[token properties](EraSpec/Contracts/BridgedStandardERC20/Properties/Model.lean),
[vault registration properties](EraSpec/Contracts/NativeTokenVaultBase/Properties/Initialization.lean),
[vault preparation properties](EraSpec/Contracts/NativeTokenVaultBase/Properties/RepresentationPreparation.lean),
and [initialization-to-preparation composition](EraSpec/Protocol/AssetBridgePreparation/Properties/Initialization.lean).

## Composing proved boundaries

[Core/Simulation.lean](EraSpec/Core/Simulation.lean) supplies generic checked
composition, with no VM or Solidity dependency:

1. A relation connects a concrete state to an abstract state.
2. Each concrete step is matched by zero or more abstract steps.
3. `Simulation.comp` composes two such boundaries.
4. `safety_transfer` transfers a reachable-state predicate, given related initial
   states, an abstract safety proof, and a relation that reflects that predicate.

There are currently **no concrete VM, Solidity, or compiler simulation instances**.
The two concrete instances are one level up, at interpreter level:
`Codegen.BridgedTokenSlice.simulation` relates the `Codegen` *interpreter* to the token
model, and `Codegen.VaultPreparationSlice.Simulation` relates the vault and token programs
to the protocol preparation trace. The first connects to Solidity through `GEN-EMIT`,
which is trusted; the second has no emitter (`MODEL-CALLS`). A further instance relates two
abstract models of the same contract: `NativeTokenVaultBase.lifecycleSimulation` simulates
the representation model's post-registration steps by registry-model steps
(`LifecycleRelIsSimulation`).
An actual instance must prove the per-step obligation; there is no global
`EVMCorrect` or `SolidityCorrect` axiom. Explicit initial-state witnesses prevent
an empty relation from establishing a deployment claim.

Forward simulation alone permits an always-reverting implementation and abstract
stuttering. Enabledness, liveness, and preservation of observable calls/returns
need further obligations. History-dependent claims require traces/ghost history
in the states or a richer observation relation. Do not use this state-only
foundation to claim byte-for-byte ABI/event equivalence.

The proposed [Lean-to-Solidity generation and shared tests](CODE_GENERATION.md)
would be another way to discharge specific correspondence boundaries. It would
not remove the VM, artifact, dependency, or environmental layers.
